A preview with a framework law is something that, of course, in every effort to make this preview as precise as possible, is based on the factors we find today. We can use factors for economic growth, for further development only under those conditions, as they exist in Austria today. I am often asked whether it makes sense at all to adopt such a path in such a fast-paced time, depending on European and international economic developments, such a framework law in Austria. – I am convinced that this makes sense because it is one of the key cornerstones for the design of politics in our country in the next few years. Of course, we depend on framework conditions. This is the economy, these are the workers, these are the very successful companies in our country, but these are also the investments of the state and the framework conditions for companies in our country, which are intended to ensure that they have an optimal opportunity to develop. These are factors such as research, development, education and much more – have long been out of the question, and of course this debate will also verify whether there are sufficient priorities in these areas. Certainly, an equally important debate, which is needed, going into depth, in the coming months, is whether these key elements, such as high employment, can be maintained in the future, i.e. strengthen strengths, low unemployment, high employment, weakness, for example, Pisa testing and other tests in our education system, can also take action in the respective budgets of the next few years. The deficit path, which still accounts for 3.9 percent in 2011, will amount to 3.3 percent of the Maastricht deficit in 2012, 2.9 percent in 2013 and 2.4 percent in 2014 and 2 percent in 2015. I believe that there is a consensus here in the House that this deficit path and therefore the lower expenditure under the heading, compared with the revenue, so that what is actually to be expected as a deficit in Maastricht, must be reduced because we do not want to endanger the triple A and the stability of our country. We know that a reduction – the reduction of revenue and expenditure to reduce the deficit – will work on the one hand if one strengthens revenue. In this respect, we have decided on a number of measures which have not yet been implemented. In addition to the well-known bank levy, there are some things that will only enter into force next year, which is why we do not yet know the revenue in detail. But, of course, there is also the second possibility, namely, in terms of expenditure, to check very carefully how the same can be achieved more efficiently in individual areas. [[Abg. Kickl: This is a parallel universe!]] Each ministry, therefore, through these requirements, which are negotiated with the Ministry of Finance, has from the outset the task of moving forward these reforms in its own field in large, small, but important steps. ParlaMint-AT_2011-05-18-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00107_d8e14737	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! I choose a less political theme, namely the situation on the labour market and the areas which have been very positively influenced by the Federal Government in recent years. In 2008, which has been mentioned several times, we had a respectable crisis caused by a financial crisis. Whoever has experienced this in real time, has seen that companies, especially in the industrial sector, have had 30, 40, even up to 60 percent of job losses. Within a few months, a very good solution was found with the social partners, but mainly also by the Federal Government, namely, short-term work. That was a very courageous decision at the time, as I must say afterwards, because the duration of the crisis was not foreseeable. The Federal Government, together with the social partners, has found the right means of overcoming the biggest crisis since the 1930s. Now, according to Eurostat, we have 4.3 percent unemployment in Austria. This is the second best value in Europe, and the Federal Financial Framework Act from 2012 to 2015 provides for approximately €6 billion for job-enhancing measures and projects. But I think that the real challenge for the next few years will be the following three themes: There is once again the demographic development with the ageing of the population, but also the financing of caretakers and the necessary level of training of the care staff. The adjustment and improvement of the structures to effectively combat wage and social dumping – a law that was first introduced – is unique in Europe, even though it is certainly necessary to demonstrate its effectiveness. The fight against youth unemployment is another important point. It is currently 9.4 percent in Austria, more than twice as high as average unemployment. However, there are countries with up to 48 percent youth unemployment. We are very good, but the issue will be a challenge, all the more so as young people deserve to be supported accordingly. However, increased attention should also be paid to the development of income in Austria. The real differences between women and men are known and measures have been taken, but according to the Court of Auditors' income report over the past twelve years, there are clear differences between workers, employees and officials. In the last 12 months, workers have earned – inflation-adjusted – minus 9 percent, employees 3.5 percent, and officials 26 percent. I think this is an issue that we should accept, and our efforts should also be reflected in income increases. As much as possible on the issue of performance justice. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-05-18-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00107_d8e20402	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Federal Minister! Mr State Secretary! High house! In principle, the agreement between the Republic of Austria and the Republic of Finland was concluded in 2000, as already mentioned. Adopted on 15 July 2000. There is now a desire for both parties to amend or amend Article 26 in particular. In essence, this amendment deals with the quality of the information exchange. At the same time, however, it is also clarified that no partner must implement administrative measures if they differ from applicable laws or the administrative practice of the Contracting State if the information cannot be obtained in accordance with the laws or in the usual administrative procedure or if a trade, industrial, commercial or professional secret would be disclosed. However, information shall be provided on request even if the requested State would not need this information for its own tax purposes. The Protocol and this Additional Protocol fulfil the international obligation of Austria to adapt its double taxation agreements to the new OECD standard on fiscal transparency and willingness to provide assistance.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-05-18-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00107_d8e20451	Mr President. Dear colleague dog-tores! Ladies and gentlemen! The issue of double taxation agreements or, quite rightly, agreements to avoid double taxation is part of a DBA network. Austria has now 82 such agreements, and these agreements are now constantly being renewed in order to comply with the OECD standard of transparency and willingness to provide assistance addressed by Mr Haberzettl. It is essentially a question of Austria withdrawing its original reservation against Article 26 of the Model Agreement. Allow me to draw a brief interim report today: We have concluded five completely new double taxation agreements with countries with which we have not yet had, but which are of great economic interest to us, such as Serbia, and we have made 14 adjustments to existing double taxation agreements. All this would not have been possible if not the head of the department, Dr. Jirousek, and his employees in the BMF had always been very persistent in these negotiations with colleagues in the finance ministries of the different countries. Austria's exports with Finland were last €500 million and our imports amounted to around €440 million. We can only see the economic significance of this question. Double taxation agreements also help to improve trade relations, which ultimately also benefits the Austrian economy, the export economy, and jobs. Exports to Finland grew by 17 percent, while imports from Finland decreased by 3.6%. However, one has to say, the mobile phone sector in particular is shrinking – all other areas are increasingly. Finland expects about 3 percent of economic growth according to the latest economic estimates. This means that if we export more than import, we can also participate in Finnish economic growth. And it is also important to have such contracts. I would also like to say the following: These agreements are also constantly being examined by the OECD Global Forum, which is an institution that checks compliance with these issues, so-called peer review, and we assume that we will also have a positive assessment. We have also taken these measures into account in a discussion with the OECD. However, one also has to say – we have often discussed that too –: It is not an abolition at the heart of Austrian banking secrecy, but it is in line with the principles of a responsible financial policy to respond with those measures which are in line with international developments, including the more existing possibilities in the field of controlling international tax fraud. In this sense, this double taxation agreement, as is certainly the case in the future, is in line with these standards.

Dr. Bartenstein.]] We Greens say: That's just not how it can be, it needs a correction! I would like to address other points which have been mentioned again today. One can also set such limits by regulation, at a different level, whatever. One doesn't necessarily have something to do with the other. Mr Dolinschek, you have also mentioned another point today: If one abolishes minor employment, i.e. also socially insured under a earnings of €374, then net is less for those affected. This can be done differently. You can work with allowances, as they already exist in unemployment insurance, because it is already the case that workers with income between € 375 and € 1 100 are exempt from unemployment insurance contributions and can nevertheless claim benefits from them. Ladies and gentlemen, the aim of the proposal is to improve the situation of 310 000 minor employees – and will continue to improve them. That's the meaning of this thing. Of course, the whole thing gets more expensive for employers, but I think that's okay. [[Abg. Dr. Bartenstein: Also for the workers, Mrs. Schatz!]] We simply can't see that there are more and more jobs from year to year that you can't live on. We cannot see that there are more and more jobs from year to year that are not covered by insurance, except for this accident insurance, which is not really social security at the end of the day. It's clear: This system of small employment simply shifts costs which employers take over from other self-employed jobs to society. That is, the public hand subsidizes these minijobs. We can't approve of this. We want full insurance for all forms of employment.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-05-18-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00107_d8e21972	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I think that the two green requests on the subject of short-term work, i.e. the requests for treasure and oillingers, are worth making a few comments on the subject of short-time work. It is also appropriate now because Mr Kickl, in the last part of his speech, referred to the subject of short-time work. The proposals that we do not agree with today – and I am going to justify them – call for a review of the granting of short-term work aid, which is open to anyone to do so. Furthermore, conditions are required for the granting of short-term work and half-yearly reports on the situation in short-time work. Mr Kickl said that we need quick solutions in short-time work, especially for small and medium-sized enterprises. How does it look in practice? – There is a review that is required. The conditions required are at the table. [[Abg. Oils: Where?]] - I'll tell you! As far as the report on the development of short work for this year is concerned, there is a solution today in the context of the applications. So there will be a report, and I believe it is also important. How does it look in practice? – The subject of review: How's that going? – It is usually the case that a farm says: I've got orders, I'm not going through, I've got to get through. Who can help me, how does it look? In general, the farmer then goes to the labour market service. This is where the social partners are being attracted. There is a meeting, if necessary a second, a third. It's talking to the people, it's talking to the farmer, it's trying to see what possibilities there are, whether short work is the best model to solve the problem there. Every problem is a little different. Or are there combinations? – A combination with education, educational care, part-time age, which is all about other offers in the AMS area. Everything is being tried to reach a solution. Or how does it look on vacations? Are there provisions, there are time resources that can be used up? All of this is being worked out in reasonable discussions; in hundreds of cases it has already happened. Of course, in some cases there is no agreement, which is clear where stop is being tried to get out as much as possible. Then you'll talk a second, third time, then it'll take a little longer. Then you look at the economic situation, then you might also look at the balance sheets to see what is reasonable to the company, because it is about continuing employment to keep workers in operation. All this is being reviewed, jointly developed, and then the short-term agreement, the social partners' agreement. This is the basis, as we all know, for providing short-term work support. That's what we're doing in terms of conditions. So what are the conditions? – A social partners agreement, usually also an operating agreement or an operating agreement on further framework conditions. It highlights the question of net wage: What actually remains if working time is reduced to half or perhaps even reduced by 90 percent? Does that mean 50 percent of the net wage for workers, that is, more or less? – There are also limits here that you try to discuss and look at. I have already said that holiday consumption is a topic, the retention time is clearly a topic. That means: Is employment security guaranteed by the company during short work and a defined period of time thereafter? – All of this plays a role as a condition for short work, and I believe that this should be seen throughout history. The topic of rental workers plays a role. Is there any in the company? How do you deal with them? Is there any short work for that? – There have been a number of agreements where employees and temporary workers, i.e. temporary workers, have made parallel short-term work agreements, because of course the temporary workers are not to be left throughout the discussion. And so on and so on. It is therefore sufficient to have conditions and inspections, and that is why we can say today that short-time work has secured a lot of jobs in the past and has cost far less than financing unemployment. That is why we do not see any particular need, since new control instruments and new bureaucracies are building up in some way. Of course, we should look at it, and we should have an overall report on this time. Thank God today – and we can also be proud of it – short-time work almost no longer has any meaning in the economy, because it is running very well, and I think we have come over quite well with this variant, that workers' side and employers' side sit together and try to find a solution.

This is the second lowest entry rate in OECD. It is easy to really work on how people can stay longer and healthier in the work process. We also know that a third of the new additions to the pension system – and that is about 30 000 people a year – go to retirement from the invalidity pension. Therefore, as has already been said, we need to keep people longer and healthier in working life. There are already efforts and successes on the part of the ministry. ParlaMint-AT_2011-05-18-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00107_d8e25274	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Only a brief consideration of my own request for the calculation of widow's and widow's pensions. In essence, this committee statement is based on a knowledge of the Constitutional Court, which says nothing but that there are severe cases, whether I base a short or a long calculation period. But I would say that a longer period of observation is socially more balanced, because deviations, so-called variations, are shortening. This is a statistical consideration, a statistical effect. You can say that twice dice brings just as much luck or luck as ten times, that's right, but the more I throw, the less easy I have very big luck or very big luck. Transferring to the legal observation period for the calculation of widow's pension is quite easy: It is better to observe ten years than two years. As a result, it is less likely to receive very low pensions. So I'm avoiding hardships. I do not need any knowledge from the Constitutional Court at all, but rather a little bit of statistics and a social conscience. Thank you.
